2.1.1. Survival analysis
The statistical analyses for mortality data will be performed separately for each sex and all
statistical tests will be conducted at the 5% significance level. The survival function of each
group will be estimated using the Kaplan Meier product limit method applied on daily intervals
and graphs will be produced. Any animal with accidental injury that causes its death or its
unscheduled sacrifice, or still alive at the day following the last day of the experimental period
will be censored in the estimation.
The log rank test will be applied to all groups in order to assess the significance of the overall
group effect on mortality data. If the result of this test is found to be significant at the 5% level,
then, using dose level scores, the significance of an overall dose-related trend in mortality and
pairwise comparisons between the reference item group and each test item treated group will
be evaluated via a Peto’s two-sided test (Peto, et al., 1980) at the 5% significance level.
Sponsor’s findings: The sponsor’s analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival
were 25 (100%), 23 (92%), 23 (92%), 25 (100%), and 5 (20%) in male mice, and 23 (92%), 25
(100%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%), and 3 (12%) in female mice in vehicle control, low, mid, high
dose groups and positive control group, respectively.
The sponsor’s analysis results were not found to be statistically significant either for males
or females.

2.1.2. Tumor data analysis
For tumor data, the statistically significant increase across doses was tested for an overall
linear dose-related increase in tumor incidence rate and pairwise comparisons using the
Cochran-Armitage’s and the Fisher’s one-sided exact tests, respectively.
Sponsor’s findings: There were no neoplastic findings attributed to daily oral gavage
administration of CK-3773274 to hemizygous transgenic rasH2 mice for 26 weeks at doses
up to 2.0 mg/kg/day.

Reference ID: 5547159

NDA219083

Page 8 of 22

2.2.

Reviewer's analyses

To verify sponsor’s analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing
pharmacologist, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses.

2.2.1. Survival analysis
The survival distributions of three treated groups, one vehical control group, and one positive
control group were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose
response relationship in survival was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity
of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for
survival rates are given in Figures 1 and 2 in the appendix for male and female mice,
respectively. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables A1 and A2 in the appendix for
male and female mice, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and
homogeneity of survivals among the vehicle and three treated groups are given in Tables A3 to
A4 in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
Reviewer’s findings: The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival
were 25 (100%), 23 (92%), 23 (92%), 25 (100%), and 5 (20%) in male mice, and 23 (92%), 25
(100%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%), and 3 (12%) in female mice in vehicle control, low, mid, high
dose groups and positive control group, respectively.
The survival analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationships in
mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups in either male or female mice.
The survival analyses showed statistically significant increase in mortality across the vehicle
control group and positive control group in both male and female mice.

2.2.2. Tumor data analysis
The tumor data were analyzed for the positive dose response relationships and pairwise
comparison between each of the treated groups control group for increased tumor incidence.
Both the dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the

Reference ID: 5547159

NDA219083

Page 9 of 22

Poly-K method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams
(1993). In this method an animal that lives the full study period ( wmax ) or dies before the
terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of sh =1. An animal

 w 
that dies at week wh without a tumor before the end of the study gets a score of sh =  h 
 wmax 
< 1. The adjusted group size is defined as Σ sh . As an interpretation, an animal with score sh

k

=1 can be considered as a whole animal while an animal with score sh < 1 can be considered
as a partial animal. The adjusted group size Σ sh is equal to N (the original group size) if all
animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice
develops at least one tumor, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted
group sizes were then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the
Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value
of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For animal
carcinogenicity K=3 is generally suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for
the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values the exact permutation method was
used.
The tumor rates and the p-values for the positive dose response relationship tests and
pairwise comparisons between vehicle control and three treated groups, and between vehicle
control and positive control are listed in Tables A7 to A8 in the appendix for male and female
mice, respectively.
Adjustment for multiple testing: For the adjustment of multiple testing of dose response
relationship for a submission with only one transgenic mouse study, the more recently
revised draft (January, 2013) FDA guidance for the carcinogenicity studies suggests the use
of test levels α =0.05 for both common tumors and rare tumors for the mouse study. For
pairwise, the same guidance document suggests the use of test levels α =0.05 for both
common tumors and rare tumors for the mouse study.
Reviewer’s findings: The tumor types in Tables 2 to 4 below show p-values less than or
equal to 0.05 in the trend test and pairwise comparisons between vehicle control and
positive control group for males and females.
1. For female mice, the tumor analysis did not show any tumor types with

Reference ID: 5547159

NDA219083

Page 10 of

22

statistically significant positive dose response relationships and statistically
significant increases in any treated group compared to the vehicle control
2. For male mice, the trend test showed statistically significant positive dose
response relationships in incidence of bronchioloalveolar adenoma, Lung (pvalue=0.0056<0.05) and combined tumors of bronchioloalveolar adenoma and
carcinoma (p-value=0.0212<0.05).
3. For male mice, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the
positive control group showed statistically significant increases in incidence of
bronchioloalveolar adenoma, lung (p-value<0.001), bronchioloalveolar carcinoma,
lung (p-value<0.001), combined tumors of bronchioloalveolar adenoma and
carcinoma, lung (p-value<0.001), hemangiosarcoma, spleen (p-value<0.001) and
combined tumors of hemangiosarcoma and hemangioma, whole body (pvalue<0.001).
4. For female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the
positive control group showed statistically significant increases in incidence of
adenoma, gland harderian (p-value=0.0052<0.05), bronchioloalveolar adenoma,

combined tumors of bronchioloalveolar adenoma and carcinoma, lung (pvalue<0.001), hemangioma, spleen (p-value=0.0498<0.05), hemangiosarcoma,
spleen (p-value<0.001) and combined tumors of hemangiosarcoma and
hemangioma, whole body (p-value<0.001).

Summary

In this submission the sponsor included the report of one animal carcinogenicity study in
Sprague Dawley rats. This study was intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of CK3773274, when administered by oral gavage once daily for 104 weeks.
Rat Study:
Survival analysis:
1. For male rats, the survival analyses showed a statistically significant dose response
relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups(p-value of
likelihood ratio test is <0.0001 and p-value of log-rank test is <0.0001). The pairwise
comparison showed a statistically positive significant difference in mortality between the
vehicle control group and the high dose group (p-value of likelihood ratio test is <0.0001
and p-value of log-rank test is <0.0001).
2. For female rats, the survival analyses showed a statistically significant dose response
relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups(p-value of
likelihood ratio test is 0.0014 and p-value of log-rank test is 0.0014). The pairwise
comparison showed a statistically positive significant difference in mortality between the
vehicle control group and the high dose group (p-value of likelihood ratio test is 0.0288 and
p-value of log-rank test is 0.0263).
Tumor analysis:
1. For male rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose
response relationship in tumor incidence across the vehicle control groups and treated
groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences
in tumor incidence between the vehicle control groups and each of the treated groups.
2. For female rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose
response relationship in tumor incidence across the vehicle control groups and treated
groups. The pairwise comparisons showed a statistically significant difference in tumor
incidence between the vehicle control groups and the low dose group (p-value=0.0221).

Reference ID: 5547154

NDA219083

Page 4 of 19

1. Background
In this submission the sponsor included the report of one animal carcinogenicity study in
Sprague Dawley rats. This study was intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of CK3773274, when administered by oral gavage once daily for 104 weeks.
In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the
effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor
incidence rate as the dose increases.

Rat Study
Two separate experiments were conducted, one in males and one in females. In each of
these two experiments there were three treated groups and one vehicle control group. Two
hundred and sixty rats of each sex were randomly assigned to the treated groups and
vehicle control group in equal size of 65 rats per group. The study was designed to continue
for up to 104 weeks for both sexes.
Due to lower survival (n=20) in males and females administered 2 and 3 mg/kg/day,
respectively, dose administration of all surviving males and females at these dose levels
was suspended as of Days 474/475 (Week 68) and 635/636 (Week 91), respectively.
Animals were maintained on study, without dosing and due to continued low survivability
(n=15), all surviving males at 2 mg/kg/day were euthanized during Weeks 79 and 80, and
surviving females at 3 mg/kg/day were euthanized during Week 96. Due to lower survival
(n=20) in control males and females, all surviving males were euthanized during Weeks 100
and 101, and all surviving females were euthanized during Week 103.

Sponsor's analyses

1.1.1. Survival analysis
Kaplan-Meier estimates of group survival rates were calculated by sex and shown graphically.
A log-rank dose response trend test of survival rates was performed utilizing ordinal
coefficients. In addition, a log-rank test for survival was used to make pairwise comparisons of
each treated group with the vehicle control group. Both the trend test and pairwise
comparisons were conducted at the 0.05 significance level.
Survival times in which the status of the animal’s death was classified as terminal euthanasia
were considered censored values for the purpose of the Kaplan-Meier estimates and survival
rate analyses.

Reference ID: 5547154

NDA219083

Page 5 of 19

Sponsor’s findings: The sponsor’s analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival
were 19 (29%), 17 (26%), 16 (25%), and 15 (23%) male rats, and 20 (31%), 24 (38%), 20 (31%),
and 15 (23%) in female rats in vehicle control, low, mid and high dose groups, respectively.
The sponsor concluded that there was a statistically significant lower survivability rate in
males administered 2 mg/kg/day and females administered 3 mg/kg/day, compared with
controls as well as a significant decreasing trend across dose groups.

1.1.2. Tumor data analysis
The incidence of tumours at different dose levels were analysed using a Peto analysis with
unadjusted p-values, following the recommendations of OECD guidance document 116.
Tumours were distinguished by their finding (e.g., carcinoma) and their organ of occurrence.
All of the tumours distinguished by finding and organ as contained in the data were
analysed. A time stratum was used in the Peto analysis for the prevalence part: The
experimental period was divided into the following time intervals : 0-52 weeks, 53-78 weeks,
79-92 weeks, over 92 weeks, and planned (terminal) sacrifice. These time intervals were
applied to time to occurrence for incidental tumours. The occurrence part for fatal tumours
of the Peto analysis used days as unit, using day of occurrence for fatal tumours. If day of
occurrence was not available, day of death was used instead.
As described in, the methodology used normal approximations which may be unreliable if
the number of tumours is small. For data sets with 10 tumours or fewer, a permutation test
was used, as implemented in SAS Version 9.4 for PROC MULTTEST. The permutation test
has also been used for data sets of 11 or more tumours.
Analyses were carried out by sex. Active treatment groups (i.e., Groups 2, 3, and 4) were
compared with the control group (i.e., Group 1) using Petos one-sided test for trends,
testing for higher incidence in the active treatment groups.
In addition to the linear trend test for all dose groups, pairwise comparisons of active
treatment groups with the control group were carried out as well, using Peto one-sided test.
Sponsor’s findings: In conclusion, there were no statistically significant tumor findings after
CK-3773274
administration to rats by oral gavage once daily for up to 704 days at doses of up to 2
mg/kg/day in males and for up to 721 days at doses of up to 3 mg/kg/day in females.

1.2.

Reviewer's analyses

To verify sponsor’s analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing
pharmacologist, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses.

1.2.1. Survival analysis
The survival distributions of three treated groups, one vehical control group, and one positive

Reference ID: 5547154

NDA219083

Page 6 of 19

control group were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose
response relationship in survival was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity
of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for
survival rates are given in Figures 1 and 2 in the appendix for male and female rats,
respectively. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 6 and 7 in the appendix for
male and female rats, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and
homogeneity of survivals among the vehicle control and three treated groups are given in
Tables 8 and 9 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
Reviewer’s findings: The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival
were 19 (29%), 17 (26%), 16 (25%), and 15 (23%) male rats, and 20 (31%), 24 (38%), 20 (31%),
and 15 (23%) in female rats in vehicle control, low, mid and high dose groups, respectively.
For male rats, the survival analyses showed a statistically significant dose response
relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups(p-value of
likelihood ratio test is <0.0001 and p-value of log-rank test is <0.0001). The pairwise
comparison showed a statistically positive significant difference in mortality between the
vehicle control group and the high dose group (p-value of likelihood ratio test is <0.0001 and
p-value of log-rank test is <0.0001).
For female rats, the survival analyses showed a statistically significant dose response
relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups(p-value of
likelihood ratio test is 0.0014 and p-value of log-rank test is 0.0014). The pairwise comparison
showed a statistically positive significant difference in mortality between the vehicle control
group and the high dose group (p-value of likelihood ratio test is 0.0288 and p-value of logrank test is 0.0263).

1.2.2. Tumor data analysis
The tumor data were analyzed for the positive dose response relationships and the positive
pairwise comparison increases between each of the treated groups with control group. Both
the dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the
Poly-K method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams
(1993). In this method an animal that lives the full study period ( wmax ) or dies before the
terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of sh =1. An animal
that dies at week wh without a tumor before the end of the study gets a score of
k

 w 
sh =  h  < 1. The adjusted group size is defined as Σ sh . As an interpretation, an animal
 wmax 
with score sh =1 can be considered as a whole animal while an animal with score sh < 1 can
be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size Σ sh is equal to N (the original

group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the
terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than
N. These adjusted group sizes were then used for the dose response relationship (or the
pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice
of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the
increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and rat studies, a value of k=3 is
suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the
calculation of p-values the exact permutation method was used.
The tumor rates and the p-values for the positive dose response relationship tests and
pairwise comparisons between vehicle control and three treated groups are listed in Tables 10,
and 11 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
Adjustment for multiple testing: For the chronic study in rats, the adjustment of multiple
testing of the dose response relationship for a submission with one chronic rat study, the
more recently revised draft (January, 2013) FDA guidance for the carcinogenicity studies
suggests the use of test levels α =0.005 for common tumors and α=0.025 for rare tumors
for the chronic rat study. For pairwise comparisonsfor the chronic rat study in the above
type of submission with one chronic rat study, the same guidance document suggests the
use of test levels α =0.01 for common tumors and α =0.05 for rare tumors for the chronic
rat study.
It should be noted that the FDA guidance for multiple testing for dose response relationship
is based on a publication by Lin and Rahman (1998). In this work the authors investigated
the use of this rule for Peto analysis. However, in a later work Rahman and Lin (2008)
showed that this rule for multiple testing for dose response relationship is also suitable for
Poly-K tests.
Reviewer’s findings:
For male rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response
relationship in tumor incidence across the vehicle control groups and treated groups. The

Reference ID: 5547154

NDA219083

Page 8 of 19

pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in tumor incidence
between the vehicle control groups and each of the treated groups.
For female rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response
relationship in tumor incidence across the vehicle control groups and treated groups. The
pairwise comparisons showed a statistically significant difference in tumor incidence between
the vehicle control groups and the low dose group (p-value=0.0221).
